#2330. 'Why are you speaking? F*** off!': Kevin De Bruyne at centre of Napoli dressing room meltdown as 'divided' squad lurches towards 'disaster'... with an 'every man for himself' culture setting in at Italian giants

Published: 03:23 EST, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 05:08 EST, 6 November 2026

Kevin De Bruyne was told to 'f*** off' repeatedly during Napoli's 4-1 thrashing against Porto on Wednesday, with a dressing room crisis heating up at the club.

The Napoli squad has become increasingly fractured in recent weeks, according to the Italian media, with an 'every man for himself' culture developing into a 'disaster'.

Napoli sit eighth in Serie A under new manager Massimiliano Allegri – just two seasons after winning the title under Antonio Conte – and are near the bottom of the Champions League table after three defeats in four games.

Their cracks are showing on the pitch, with striker Lorenzo Lucca yelling at De Bruyne: 'Why are you speaking? F*** off!' during their Porto drubbing.

The pair squared up to each other and Lucca shouted his expletive command three times before they went their separate ways.

After the match, Matteo Politano told Sky Sport Italia: 'It would be nice if a few other teammates came here to give an explanation and not just us Italians, but never mind, I never hide my face.'

Kevin De Bruyne was told to 'f*** off' three times by team-mate Lorenzo Lucca during Napoli's 4-1 defeat by Porto on Wednesday

There is said to be a rift developing between Napoli's native stars and foreign players

La Gazzetta dello Sport reported that Politano was calling out Cameroonian star Frank Anguissa and Kosovan Amir Rrahmani, both two-time title winners with the club.

Politano added: 'The attitude is one that a team like ours cannot have at this level. I was not attacking anyone, we have a squad that is struggling, but we need to come together and think about this next game before the break for international duty.

'Hopefully we can recover a few players during that spell and then our season can really begin.'

There is a 'divide' between the Italian group of players and foreigners, La Gazzetta dello Sport reports, though it is mostly down to frustration with results.

Individuals such as De Bruyne, Rasmus Hojlund, and Stanislav Lobotka are more 'reserved personalities' who have reportedly not bonded as well with their team-mates away from the field.

Scott McTominay, who has an ally in fellow Scottish player Billy Gilmour, said after the Porto loss: 'Of course I’m angry.

'We lost 4-1 in the Champions League; there’s nothing to smile about. For us, it’s go on and try to figure out how to fix it.

'We’ve had a lot of injuries, a lot of important players you can’t change; attacking players as well. Let’s not beat around the bush; we’ve got a lot of injuries, and that makes a big difference whenever you come into play every three days.

Napoli have had a rough start to the season under new manager Massimiliano Allegri - despite him being a legendary coach in Italy

'So, things like these can make a huge difference for the team when the same players are playing over and over again. People need to understand that, but we also need to do better. First and foremost, we need to do better, injuries in football are part of it, but we have too many.'

Their dressing room troubles have dragged on for a while. Last season, Conte complained of a lack of unity after they signed nine players on the back of their Scudetto. They still managed to finish second, but it was a turbulent ride.

'Nine new players were too many. We weren't balanced,' he said at the time.

'I experience the dressing room every day, so I say certain things; we mustn't despair. We'll try to recreate last year's chemistry.'

In one match against AC Milan, De Bruyne threw a tantrum after being subbed off by Conte.

#2329. « Mon amour pour ce sport reste intact » : Poussé dehors par Haas, Esteban Ocon publie un message d'adieu très classe sur Instagram

Mis à la porte par l'écurie Haas avec effet immédiat, Esteban Ocon a pris la parole ce mardi 10 novembre 2026. Le pilote français a tenu à adresser un message sobre à ses supporters ainsi qu'à l'ensemble du paddock de la Formule 1.

Cette prise de parole intervient une semaine après l'officialisation de son éviction, actée le mardi 3 novembre 2026 pour les quatre derniers Grands Prix de la saison 2026. Une rupture brutale alors qu'Esteban Ocon, 30 ans, occupait la 16e place du classement des pilotes avec 7 points, contre 20 points pour son coéquipier Oliver Bearman.

Sur son compte Instagram, le vainqueur du Grand Prix de Hongrie 2021 a publié un texte sobre, sans la moindre rancœur envers sa direction. Esteban Ocon a préféré remercier chaleureusement les personnes qui l'ont soutenu durant cette épreuve, rappelant son attachement indéfectible pour sa discipline.

Le pilote tricolore compte 199 engagements et 198 départs en Formule 1, ainsi que 4 podiums en carrière.

L'aventure s'est pourtant terminée dans la tourmente. En effet, tout s'est accéléré le samedi 31 octobre 2026 lors du Grand Prix de Mexico sur le circuit de l'Autódromo Hermanos Rodríguez. Mis à l'écart soudainement après un différend interne lors des essais libres du vendredi, le Français avait alors cédé sa place au réserviste japonais Ryo Hirakawa. Un coup dur.

Une séparation était déjà programmée fin septembre 2026, mais l'accord prévoyait initialement qu'il termine la saison. Toutefois, le directeur de l'écurie, Ayao Komatsu, a affirmé avoir pris cette décision anticipée dans le meilleur intérêt de la structure. Haas a d'ailleurs aligné le rookie brésilien Rafael Câmara, déjà engagé pour 2027, dès le Grand Prix de São Paulo le week-end du 8 novembre 2026.

Désormais sur la touche, Esteban Ocon fait face à un futur incertain. À trois manches de la fin de la saison 2026, il ne dispose d'aucun baquet titulaire confirmé pour 2027. Au-delà des doutes contractuels, sa passion et son amour pour la Formule 1 restent aussi forts que jamais.

Le prochain rendez-vous du championnat du monde de Formule 1 2026 sera le Grand Prix de Las Vegas, 21e manche de la saison, programmé du 19 au 21 novembre 2026 sur le Las Vegas Strip Circuit.

#2324. Klopp's striker hierarchy revealed as Arsenal star Havertz leads the way

Four games into Jürgen Klopp's tenure as Germany coach, Bild has published what it claims is the internal ranking of forwards in the German national team.

According to the German tabloid, Arsenal's Kai Havertz is the clear number one, with Klopp valuing the former Bayer Leverkusen attacker's intelligence, movement and experience at the highest level.

Rather surprisingly, Eintracht Frankfurt's Younes Ebnoutalib currently sits second in the rankings. The 23-year-old made his first two appearances for Germany in September and, although he was unable to really convince, his impressive early-season form has apparently propelled him high up the pecking order.

The same applies to third-placed Niclas Füllkrug, who is on loan at Werder Bremen from West Ham. Having missed Klopp's first squad, the striker was handed a call-up this time around after climbing to the top of the Bundesliga scoring charts with nine goals.

Jonathan Burkardt, Nick Woltemade and Deniz Undav complete the hierarchy in fourth, fifth and sixth place respectively, having all missed out on the squad this time around.

Should Havertz, Ebnoutalib or Füllkrug pick up an injury, Burkardt would be first in line for a call-up as the next-ranked striker.

#2322. Australian football rocked by allegations players fixed matches and illegally bet on their own games

Published: 05:28 EST, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 05:28 EST, 6 November 2026

Football Australia is investigating allegations that players registered with an Adelaide soccer club manipulated matches and illegally gambled on their own games during the 2026 season.

The Cove FC has responded to the reports by stating the club itself is not under investigation, and that the allegations concern 'a very small number of individuals' registered with the club.

The Trott Park club, in Adelaide's southern suburbs, released a statement on Friday after reports emerged of the integrity probe.

Football SA has confirmed an investigation involving players registered with Cove during the 2026 season, with Football Australia handling the matter.

'The Club wishes to make clear that The Cove FC itself is not under investigation,' chairman Phil Hole said in the statement.

'The allegations concern a very small number of individuals registered with the Club during the 2026 season.'

Players registered with The Cove FC during the 2026 season are the subject of an ongoing Football Australia integrity investigation

The probe reportedly concerns allegations of match-fixing and illegal betting on players' own games

According to News Corp, the allegations involve competition manipulation and illegal betting on the players' own matches.

No individuals or specific fixtures have been publicly identified in the supplied statements, and no findings or penalties have been announced.

Football Australia told 10 News Adelaide that its disciplinary process was ongoing and it would not comment further while its integrity unit worked through the matter.

Hole said Cove had cooperated with the national governing body throughout the investigation.

'The Cove FC takes matters of sporting integrity extremely seriously and has cooperated promptly and fully with Football Australia since becoming aware of the allegations and throughout the investigative process,' he said.

'The Club respects the ongoing investigative process and will not comment further at this stage.'

Football SA said Football Australia had exclusive jurisdiction to investigate the alleged integrity breaches, and that the state body would assist if requested.

'Cove FC have been fully cooperative and supportive of the investigation by Football Australia,' Football SA said.

Chairman Phil Hole says Cove has cooperated fully with Football Australia throughout the investigative process

'While the investigation remains active, Football SA is unable to provide details about the allegations or the parties involved.'

Daily Mail has contacted South Australian Police for comment.

Founded in 1983, Cove operates from the Southern Soccer Facility at Trott Park and has more than 800 members.

Its senior teams compete at semi-professional level, while the club also provides junior, women's, amateur, masters and inclusive football programs.

Hole said the club remained focused on its members and community while the investigation continued.

'We remain firmly committed to the values, standards and sporting integrity that underpin our Club,' he said.

'Our focus remains on our members, our community and building on the positive progress and success of The Cove FC.'

#2320. Sabatini: “Il Napoli ha giocato le tre partite più spettacolari della stagione”

Il giornalista Sandro Sabatini, attraverso un video pubblicato sul proprio canale YouTube, ha analizzato il pareggio per 1-1 tra Napoli e Lazio: “Le tre partite più belle, spettacolari, ricche di gol, emozioni e occasioni di questa stagione sono state Inter-Napoli 3-2, Napoli-Roma 3-3 e Napoli-Lazio 1-1. Il calcio dominante, propositivo e moderno si è visto in queste tre partite del Napoli, che sono state tra le migliori di un campionato che, magari dopo la sosta, potrà riservare qualche correzione. È proprio il Napoli di Allegri ad augurarsi questa correzione, perché la classifica parla chiaro. Nonostante alcune prestazioni all’altezza della situazione, gli azzurri si ritrovano staccatissimi, intorno al decimo posto, con un divario obiettivamente incolmabile per un’eventuale lotta scudetto e quasi incolmabile persino per il quarto posto. Il calcio è così, è paradossale. Non sempre si riesce a trovare una conferma o una chiave di lettura per qualsiasi partita, a meno che non ci si voglia lasciare guidare dalle ideologie e dai commenti precostituiti, anziché dalle idee e dall’analisi. Fatta questa premessa, Napoli e Lazio hanno pareggiato 1-1 al termine di una partita molto emozionante, spettacolare e combattuta. Il primo tempo è stato maggiormente favorevole alla Lazio, che infatti lo ha concluso in vantaggio per 1-0. Nella ripresa il Napoli ha trovato il pareggio, propiziato da McTominay e Hojlund, con la complicità di Pedraza, autore dell’autogol. Gli azzurri hanno poi costruito altre occasioni, neutralizzate dal portiere. Su tutte, quella clamorosa nel duello faccia a faccia con De Bruyne. Sì, il Napoli nel secondo tempo avrebbe potuto anche vincere, ma la Lazio è una squadra solida, che merita il primo posto in classifica. Gattuso l’ha organizzata benissimo. Mi è piaciuto tantissimo il primo tempo della Lazio, ma anche la gestione degli ultimi dieci minuti, nei quali ha giocato da grande squadra. Bisogna considerare che anche la Lazio, così come il Napoli, ha dovuto fare i conti con assenze importanti. A un certo punto è stata costretta a rinunciare a Sutalo e Nuno Tavares, oltre a dover fare a meno di Zaccagni. Tre assenze pesanti, eppure la Lazio esce dal Maradona tra la soddisfazione dei propri tifosi. I sostenitori della Lazio di Gattuso festeggiano, ed è giusto che sia così. Quelli del Napoli di Allegri, invece, se la prendono con tutti, a cominciare dall’allenatore, per arrivare a quello che sembra essere diventato l’imputato numero uno. Imputato di che cosa, poi, non si capisce bene. Pensate che Noa Lang, da un certo momento in avanti, è stato fischiato per tutta la partita. Eppure Noa Lang è stato nettamente il migliore in campo del Napoli. Un giocatore vivo, sempre pronto a puntare l’uomo. Romano Floriani Mussolini, alla sua prima presenza da titolare, ha vissuto una giornata difficilissima, tanto da essere sostituito da Lazzari, perché nessuno riusciva a fermare Lang. Eppure i tifosi del Napoli lo fischiavano. Per che cosa? Per un gesto? Per una sorta di regolamento di conti? Non si capisce. Credo sia stato qualcosa che non dovrebbe trovare spazio nel giudizio su Lang. Naturalmente i tifosi possono fare quello che vogliono, ma il rendimento di un calciatore di personalità, come lo è Lang, un folle ma dotato di personalità, non dipende certo dai fischi o dagli applausi. Il rendimento di un giocatore o di una squadra non dipende dagli applausi o dai fischi dello stadio. E la dimostrazione, per certi versi, arriva proprio dalla Lazio. Mi dispiace dirlo, perché la passione dei tifosi laziali, compresi quelli che non vanno allo stadio, è fuori discussione. Però la dimostrazione che il pubblico conta fino a un certo punto arriva proprio dalla stagione della Lazio, che gioca in uno stadio quasi deserto eppure è in testa al campionato. Una squadra che sembrava destinata a lottare per non retrocedere sta invece lottando per lo scudetto. Vedremo quali saranno gli altri risultati, ma una cosa è sicura: chi, dopo queste prime giornate e le prime due soste, esclude la Lazio dalla lotta scudetto, secondo me commette un errore. È una squadra cresciuta con Gattuso, così come è cresciuto lo stesso allenatore, tornato, secondo me, alla semplicità, alla razionalità, alla precisione, al lavoro e alla solidità del suo primo staff, senza tutte le confusioni dell’esperienza in Nazionale. Quindi complimenti alla Lazio, ma per una volta complimenti anche al Napoli. Nel Napoli, Milinkovic-Savic ha parato quello che doveva parare, poi è stato impallinato da Frattesi. Sul gol del primo tempo il pallone gli passa praticamente accanto alla faccia. Per il resto è stato preciso negli interventi. Beukema un po’ meglio rispetto alle precedenti esibizioni, anche se non ci voleva molto. Rrahmani si vede che sta tirando avanti, lottando nonostante una condizione fisica complicata e dolori che potrebbero anche metterlo in difficoltà. Eppure continua a combattere con grande leadership. Rafa Marín discreto. Aveva offerto qualcosa di meglio nel corso della stagione, ma la sua resta una prestazione sufficiente. Olivera corre più di tutti, soltanto che poi sbaglia più di tutti sotto porta. È un giocatore che, se andassimo a vedere gli xG di questo campionato, probabilmente scopriremmo essere andato vicino al gol diverse volte, anche con salvataggi sulla linea. Poverino, non riesce mai a trovare la soddisfazione della rete. Nel secondo tempo ha avuto un pallone da calciare con il destro, che non è il suo piede. Era quasi un rigore, leggermente decentrato, ma lo ha mandato oltre la traversa. McTominay molto bene nel secondo tempo, così come De Bruyne, soprattutto quando i due si sono invertiti di posizione. Gilmour geometrico, didascalico e niente più. Vergara è partito molto male, ma ha concluso meglio. A me piace anche senza i gol, perché è un giocatore che, quando troverà maggiore fiducia, autostima e leggerezza mentale, potrà diventare molto più incisivo. E poi c’è Lang. È un giocatore che può dividere. Può risultare simpatico o, più probabilmente, antipatico. Può essere considerato un professionista serio, anche se non credo sia questa l’immagine che trasmette, oppure un folle, uno scapestrato, un genialoide, un artista, un rapper. Può essere considerato tutto. Ma se i tifosi del Napoli fischiano Lang per aver fatto quel gesto, significa che la competenza calcistica viene superata dall’umore, dall’invidia o da qualcosa che non ha nulla a che fare con il calcio e, se posso permettermi, nemmeno con il sostegno di cui questa squadra ha bisogno. Lo dico per i tifosi del Napoli, ma anche per quelli dell’Inter, della Roma, per quelli che l’anno scorso contestavano le proprie squadre e per quelli della Fiorentina. Se si va allo stadio concentrandosi sul fischiare un giocatore e sul trovare un capro espiatorio, come è successo anche a Lucca nell’ultimo quarto d’ora, va bene, si può fare tutto. I tifosi ne hanno il diritto, ma l’appoggio è un’altra cosa. Ed è proprio l’appoggio che i tifosi dovrebbero dare. Per me Lang è un giocatore di livello internazionale. È ovvio che non giocherà tutte le partite come questa, ma le sue qualità non si discutono. Concludendo, la Lazio è in corsa per lo scudetto. Il Napoli, se dovesse continuare a giocare così, potrebbe tornare a lottare per un posto tra le prime quattro. Ma deve continuare su questa strada”.

#2319. Serbia vs. Germany preview: Team news and predicted lineups

Germany will be looking to put themselves in the strongest possible position ahead of a potential Group A2 decider against the Netherlands next week when they travel to Belgrade to face Serbia on Friday at 20:45 in their penultimate 2026/27 UEFA Nations League group-stage fixture.

The two teams met last month, with Germany taking a 2-0 win at home thanks to goals from Tom Bischof and Florian Wirtz.

That victory remains Germany's only win under Jürgen Klopp, with Die Nationalmannschaft currently sitting third in Group A2 on five points, behind the Netherlands and Greece on eight points and ahead of bottom-placed Serbia, who have yet to pick up a point.

Last month, Serbia were ordered to play the upcoming game against Germany behind closed doors after their fans engaged in racist and discriminatory behaviour during matches against Greece and the Netherlands.

However, Friday's game at the Rajko Mitić Stadium in Belgrade will not be played entirely without spectators.

As the Serbian Football Association confirmed to DPA upon request, a clause in UEFA regulations will allow children under the age of 15 to attend the match, provided they are accompanied by teachers or coaches, with around 5,000 expected to be in attendance.

After calling up a total of 44 players for his first four games as Germany coach, Klopp has named a more streamlined 24-man squad this time around. Borussia Mönchengladbach's Wael Mohya, VfB Stuttgart's Finn Jeltsch and RB Leipzig's Brajan Gruda could potentially make their Germany debuts.

Klopp also notably opted to bring Werder Bremen's Niclas Füllkrug back into the squad, with the West Ham loanee currently topping the Bundesliga scoring charts with nine league goals.

Saša Lukić and Nemanja Maksimović are both suspended for Serbia, while Aleksandar Stanković, Andrej Ilić, Strahinja Pavlović and Adem Avdić are set to miss out through injury.

Germany, meanwhile, will be without Jamal Musiala, Serge Gnabry, Waldemar Anton, Assan Ouédraogo and Angelo Stiller due to injuries.

Said El Mala sat out Germany's training session on Tuesday, but his absence is likely to have been part of a workload-management programme, with the 1. FC Köln star is expected to be fit for the match.

#2318. Mum rushes to goalkeeper in Spanish football's killer collision: Jesus Owono suffering 'hardest days of his life' and being comforted after striker he clashed with died from 'chest trauma'

Published: 05:24 EST, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 05:29 EST, 6 November 2026

Goalkeeper Jesus Owono's doting mum has rushed to Andorra to comfort him as he endures the 'hardest days of his life' following his death clash with a rival striker whose funeral took place yesterday.

Maria del Carmen Owono Mangue made the five-and-a-half-hour drive from her home in the Basque city of San Sebastian to Andorra where her 25-year-old son currently plays his football after learning Jesus de Miguel had lost his fight for life in a Tenerife hospital.

She and the Andorra FC keeper have been deeply close since he moved to San Sebastian from their native Equatorial Guinea as an eight-year-old boy to rejoin her after she had emigrated earlier in search of work to provide for her family and an aunt had taken the youngster into her home.

Owono, the eldest of six siblings, has admitted of his arrival in a new country: 'I came in pursuit of a dream, not the dream of playing football, but the dream of being with my mother.'

His mum has confessed: 'Waking up in the morning and seeing your child doing what they love most is a mother's greatest joy.'

She said in the same interview when the goalkeeper was just 20 in comments which could now come back to haunt the family: 'He needs to keep working. We still don't know what will happen. If God allows things to turn out well for him and if not, well, you know that he has worked hard and done what he could.'

Goalkeeper Jesus Owono is suffering and being comforted after his killer collision

Owono, who has closed his social media accounts, has had his mother rush to his side in the days following Jesus de Miguel's death (Source: Instagram)

Overnight one of Jesus' younger brothers confirmed their mum was by his side in Andorra.

Speaking from the second-floor four-bed apartment she owns in a tree-lined street in a middle-class residential neighbourhood east of San Sebastian's city centre, he said: 'We're all very distraught. My mum has gone to be with Jesus because he is suffering.'

A source close to the goalkeeper added: 'Maria del Carmen is one of the most important, if not the most important, people in Jesus' life.

'He had a difficult start being separated from his mum at such an early age and it was very difficult for her too.

'They have been inseparable since Jesus moved to Spain as an eight-year-old.

'Maria del Carmen has helped him integrate into completely new surroundings and supported him every step of the way on his road to becoming a professional footballer.

De Miguel and Owono collided - and hours later the former died of 'accidental chest trauma'

De Miguel's team-mates are pictured arriving at his funeral in Navahermosa on Thursday

'It's only natural that she'd want to be by his side now when by his own admission he's in pieces and finding it impossible to come to terms with the fact a fellow footballer has died because of something he did.'

From the FC Andorra supporters' section, we want to send you all our support and love during these tough and difficult times.

We are aware of the suffering you are going through after what happened. We know these aren't easy days, but we want you to remember one thing: you are not alone.

You have your family, your friends, your team-mates, the staff, and the entire club by your side. But you also have us, a fanbase that appreciates you, respects you, and today, more than ever, wants to send you all our love and support.

We also want you to know that we categorically reject all the insults, threats, and judgments you've been receiving lately. An accident doesn't define a person, much less their character.

We don't want the pain, anger, or helplessness to turn into hatred towards you. Behind the footballer is a person, with feelings and a family who are also suffering.

We want you to know that this is your home. When you return to the pitch, to defend our goal and wear this jersey again, you'll have a stand that will still be by your side, cheering you on as always.

Now you don't have to prove anything to anyone. It won't be easy and it will take time, but even though it's difficult and painful, we have to move forward, we have to work to recover that fighting spirit and smile that are so characteristic of you. The most important thing now is your recovery, but along the way, know that you can count on us, that you're not alone.

Stay strong, Jesus. We're with you. Our Spider Man today, tomorrow, and always.

Some of Owono's Andorra FC team-mates and members of the club's supporters' group have also paid him a visit to deliver a letter of encouragement.

The letter refers to the goalkeeper as 'their Spiderman,' 'his joy,' and 'a person with feelings and a strong family connection.'

Owono was red-carded after kneeing the 30-year-old CD Tenerife striker in the chest in a reckless tackle late into stoppage time during a second-division match on Monday.

The match was suspended when the two sides were drawing 2-2.

Tragic Jesus, whose partner Marta Fernandez and one-year-old daughter Carla were watching from the stands, was reportedly revived by paramedics on the pitch after going into cardiac arrest before being rushed to hospital.

His club confirmed around 9.30am local time on Tuesday he had died about 8am the same day, saying they were 'heartbroken' and had a 'lump in their throats' after revealing earlier the footballer's neurological condition had worsened.

A post-mortem concluded in a preliminary report his death was consistent with 'accidental chest trauma' although the results of further tests conducted during the autopsy are still pending.

Owono used 'excessive force' while contesting the ball near the half-way line according to the match referee's report.

He is facing a lengthy ban and possible criminal charges after police in Tenerife confirmed homicide squad officers were looking into whether had committed an involuntary manslaughter.

Legal experts have cast doubt on the possibility of prosecution because the clash that resulted in Jesus de Miguel's death occurred during open play.

The first photos of the dead footballer's partner emerged overnight as his team-mates prepared to travel to the small town of Navahermosa, 80 miles south-west of Madrid, where his devastated parents come from.

Jesus' mum and dad made the return trip after jetting to Tenerife to try to reach their son before he died in hospital on Tuesday morning hours after his clash with Owono.

The father was watching the match in a bar in his home town when he saw him slump to the ground.

Equatorial Guinea international Owono, who turned round as he headed back to the dressing rooms following his sending off and walked back to the player he had hurt after realising how seriously injured the Tenerife frontman was, broke his silence on Wednesday.

The father of De Miguel, is hugged at his son's wake, three days on from his death

The football world has rallied behind De Miguel and his family with tributes to the striker

A neighbour said De Miguel's father (pictured right, with his mother, girlfriend and sister R-L) burst into tears after seeing the impact of the collision on television. (Source: Instagram/martitamig)

He released a statement via his club that said: 'These are the most difficult days of my life.

'I am struggling to find the words, and it is very hard for me to process anything that has happened.

'I feel immense pain over the loss of Jesus. I am thinking of him, his family, and all the people who loved him.

'I know there will be many matters to address, and I will face whatever comes with complete respect.

'But right now, honestly, I cannot think of anything other than Jesus and the grief we are experiencing. Rest in peace, Jesus.'

FC Andorra, reacting to news of the Tenerife striker's death in hospital, said on Tuesday morning: 'Heartbroken and devastated, we wish to extend our deepest condolences to the family of Jesus de Miguel, all his colleagues and friends, and to CD Tenerife.

Ci crediamo, siamo una grande squadra, e potremo ottenere risultati contro qualunque squadra. Sappiamo che in Europa è un gioco diverso".

"Sì, è verò, è una competizione diversa, la qualità è più alta, i più piccoli errori possono portare a un gol. Non è come in Serie A, dove puoi essere più tranquillo. Qui se fai un passaggio sbagliato può arrivare un gol. Dobbiamo essere pronti e imparare. Credo che cresceremo come squadra e come calciatrici. È una buona esperienza, ma vogliamo di più".

Per concludere, in cosa dovete migliorare nelle prossime due partite di Champions League?

"Sì, è una buona domanda. Dobbiamo guardare il gioco, impararlo e concentrarci sulla prossima partita, quella di domenica. Poi ci sarà il Real Madrid la settimana prossima: è bello avere molti partite, perché è quello che vogliamo".

#2314. "Far too good" – German transfer insider weighs in on Bayern links to Arsenal star Martin Ødegaard

Towards the end of October, Sky Germany reported that Arsenal's Martin Ødegaard could envisage a move to Bayern Munich, raising the possibility of a move should Arsenal fail to agree a new deal with their captain beyond 2028.

However, Bild head of football Christian Falk has now played down the speculation, suggesting that the 27-year-old is "too old and far too good" for a move to the German champions to be a realistic option.

“He’s now too old and far too good for it to be an option for FC Bayern – especially at a time when the club is already fully stocked in this position,” Falk said in quotes published on his CfBayern Insider website.

The German journalist added that Bayern did show genuine interest in Ødegaard when he was still young and making his way through the ranks.

“Remember, when he was very, very young (I think about 16 or so), he was at Säbener Straße for a training session week when Pep Guardiola was the manager. At this point, it was very interesting for FC Bayern to sign the player, but they didn’t get him in the end," he said, before adding:

“It’s always nice to see such names connected with the Bundesliga giants, but he won’t be coming to Bayern any time soon.”

#2310. "I might have deserved the opportunity a little more" – Ter Stegen opens up on rivalry with Neuer

Heading into Germany's upcoming games against Serbia and the Netherlands, Marc-André ter Stegen has been named as Jürgen Klopp undisputed first choice in goal.

For the 34-year-old, this marks a welcome change after years of playing second fiddle to Manuel Neuer and struggling with injuries.

In an interview with Sport Bild, ter Stegen looks back on his long-standing rivalry with the now 40-year-old Bayern Munich shot-stopper, who announced his international retirement for good after last summer's World Cup.

While ter Stegen clearly has huge respect for Neuer's qualities, he makes no secret of the fact that he would have liked to play more than the 45 games he has featured in for Germany at senior level.

“Manuel Neuer is not simply a great goalkeeper. We don't need to fool ourselves about that: he is probably one of the best goalkeepers of all time. He also had an enormous standing within the national team, and rightly so. However, he was also held back by injuries,” ter Stegen said.

“And who knows more about that than I do, unfortunately? Nevertheless, I believe a certain level of competition pushes the goalkeeper who gets to play to improve. That's how it was with us.

“Even so, the question naturally remains: would I rather have been playing? Of course! But the coaches we had with the national team chose Manu. You have to respect that, and I have to accept it. That's simply part of sport.”

The 34-year-old did admit that there were times when he felt he deserved to play ahead of Neuer, despite not being given the opportunity.

“Did I sometimes have to bite my tongue? Did I sometimes swear under my breath when nobody was listening? Certainly! There were also moments when I felt I might have deserved the opportunity a little more,” he said.

One particularly bitter moment for ter Stegen came at the 2018 World Cup, when Neuer managed to return from a long-term injury and reclaim his place in the starting line-up ahead of him despite lacking match practice.

“That was the most bitter moment, but I don't want to revisit it. The fact remains that the coach (Jogi Löw at the time, ed.) makes the decision. You can get as angry as you like,” said ter Stegen, who is currently Ajax's first-choice goalkeeper while on loan from FC Barcelona.

#2309. Why Enzo Maresca is sticking with 'awe-inspiring' Gianluigi Donnarumma despite Leipzig horror show, growing catalogue of errors and a worrying downward trend

Published: 12:00 EST, 5 November 2026 | Updated: 07:07 EST, 6 November 2026

There was a Manchester City fan on Thursday’s Ryanair flight back home from Leipzig wearing a Gianluigi Donnarumma sticker on his coat, portraying the Italian as Don Vito Corleone. The sticker, a bit worn and a bit crumpled, was peeling off. A metaphor exists within that somewhere.

Tough times for Donnarumma, a calamitous night in the Champions League – beaten at his near post by Ridle Baku's cross for one goal, fumbling another routine effort at the feet of a striker and allowing a straightforward shot to squirm through his fingers – just adding to the feeling that the past few weeks have not reflected kindly on him.

Question marks around Donnarumma’s suitability between the sticks are becoming more pronounced after a catalogue of mistakes this season. One against Paris Saint-Germain when Maghnes Akliouche’s shot was too hot (some would argue two after neglecting his near post for Desire Doue’s opener) and then Nicolas Jackson shooting through him at Aston Villa last month.

Each time, Enzo Maresca has publicly recognised the errors and intimated that nobody needs to rake over them in detail with his No1, who is experienced enough to know he must do better.

Now Leipzig. ‘It’s not a concern,’ Maresca said of the latest gaffe. The manager’s defences of Donnarumma are racking up and what City are witnessing is fundamental problems from somebody described as the best the world has to offer.

Maresca has rejected the idea that summer signing Geronimo Rulli, a 34-year-old Argentina international, could take over the gloves, claiming that City line-ups are always ‘Gigio plus 10’. Rulli understood his lot when joining from Marseille, to feature in domestic cup competitions, and was happy with that.

Gianluigi Donnarumma had a night to forget in Leipzig this week, making at least three individual errors

But Man City boss Enzo Maresca will stand by his goalkeeper, claiming that City line-ups are always ‘Gigio plus 10’

Happy in a way that James Trafford was not, after making it clear that one season as Donnarumma’s understudy was not palatable.

He has flourished at Leeds United so far this season, leading to some observers criticising Hugo Viana for letting him go, only to blunder for a goal at Bournemouth last weekend. Worth remembering that it does happen to all of them at some point.

Trafford did not appear ready for the big time last year when starting the season ahead of the wantaway Ederson and City had their eyes on Donnarumma months before landing him – a £26million deal struck just as Ederson left for Fenerbahce.

Trafford will be ready soon and is pushing Jordan Pickford hard to be England's No1, but definitely wasn’t back then.

With Trafford gone and Maresca seemingly ruling out his No2 taking over – while also making a vague reference to taking stock over the international break – it appears that City would have no option but to stick with Donnarumma if they did actually want to bring about a change. January signings seem remote in the current climate of guilty verdicts and appeals.

But there is something outstanding that Donnarumma does offer, one that does set him apart. Maresca categorised the stunning one-handed save from Leipzig’s Christoph Baumgartner as one nobody else could make.

And while some hyperbole might be at play there, very few could lay claim to producing that kind of moment on a semi-regular basis.

Repelling Baumgartner is up there with Donnarumma’s instinctive save from Bryan Mbeumo at a crucial juncture on debut last year and thwarting Alexis Mac Allister in a victory at Anfield. The sort of awe-inspiring saves thousands pay money to witness.

Very few goalkeepers can claim to make wonder-saves as regularly as Donnarumma does

Donnarumma's impressive shot-stopping in the Premier League is evidenced by this graphic of every shot he has faced since joining City (red circles = goals, blank circles = saves)

Nobody across the Premier League has a higher save percentage than Donnarumma’s 71.5 since his arrival from PSG. More scientifically, only Bart Verbruggen and Senne Lammens have prevented more goals in that time – a metric measured by the xG of shots compared to how many goals were conceded.

Donnarumma has prevented seven goals (1.4 this season), with Brighton’s Verbruggen at 9.7 and Manchester United's Lammens at 8.1 across the same period. These are broadly encouraging numbers for City, despite Donnarumma's save percentage dipping to 69.7 this season – eighth among his peers, with Trafford way out in front on 79.5.

City will need to keep a careful eye on that downward trend from a 27-year-old who they brought to the club to act as the anchor, to bail them out in a way that Ederson struggled. That was the trade-off for his limited footwork, being incapable of playing through the opposition lines in a way the Brazilian could.

The wonder saves cancel all of that out. Donnarumma has been the goalkeeper’s goalkeeper for those alone. But were the certainty around that aspect of his game to waver on a longer-term basis, City should have decisions to make.

#2305. Füllkrug addresses age concerns after returning to Germany squad under Klopp

Topping the Bundesliga's scoring charts with nine goals for Werder Bremen, Niclas Füllkrug has been recalled to the Germany squad by Jürgen Klopp for the upcoming UEFA Nations League fixtures against Serbia on Friday and the Netherlands on Monday.

A few weeks before announcing his latest squad, Klopp seemed to suggest that Füllkrug's age of 33 was a disadvantage as he looks to build a Germany team for the future.

“Niclas Füllkrug scores goal after goal in the Bundesliga, but he’s 33. That’s not my fault," Klopp said in an interview with DAZN last month.

“We have the present, the medium-term future and the long-term future. In the present, it can of course work; in the medium term, possibly as well. Long term, well, we’ll have to see.”

While Klopp has since admitted that he is “not thrilled” with the statements he made in that interview, Füllkrug himself addressed his age at a press conference on Monday.

“My age is a fact, but it’s not something I think about too much. I’ve spoken to the head coach about it and I didn’t take his comments personally in the slightest," he said.

"We’re at a stage where the focus is on developing young players. We’ve got several players who are really making an impression at the moment, so there are plenty of options to choose from.”

Overall, the West Ham loanee is simply happy to be back in the squad for the first time in over a year.

“I’m delighted to be back. It’s recognition of the work you put in and how you perform for your club. It’s great to be part of the national team again and to have the chance to train under Jürgen Klopp," he said.

"I’ve caught up with a lot of familiar faces and met plenty of new ones too. The squad looks very different now and you can really feel how much has changed. I’m happy that I can just be myself and that people respond positively to that.”

#2304. Inter, oggi nuovi esami per Thuram: i tempi di recupero. Bonny più grave, la situazione Stankovic

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Dopo la vittoria col Como, non sono arrivate buone notizie per l'Inter dall'infermeria. Mentre Bonny non è partito per un problema muscolare, rientreranno invece Marcus Thuram e Aleksandar Stankovic.

"Non c’è stato il tempo materiale per applaudire il countdown di Lautaro, pronto a tornare in gruppo a partire da lunedì: l’Inter deve fronteggiare altri due infortuni in attacco. Uno sembra lieve e riguarda Marcus Thuram, visitato dallo staff medico della nazionale francese e rispedito subito a Milano. Oggi farà altri esami con lo staff guidato dal dottor Volpi ma le indiscrezioni a distanza parlano di un semplice affaticamento muscolare. Niente di così grave da pregiudicare la sua stagione: potrebbe mostrare il pollice alzato a Cristian Chivu già per la prossima partita contro l’Atalanta, il 22 novembre a Bergamo. È più grave il problema di Ange-Yoan Bonny, che si è bloccato nell’ultima azione della partita contro il Como. È stata la classica “pizzicata” dietro alla coscia, una lesione nemmeno leggerissima ai flessori che lo estrometterà dalle partite per un mese. Niente nazionale anche per lui, che la Costa d’Avorio ha legittimato a non partire invano per il raduno", scrive La Gazzetta dello Sport.

Published: 08:02 EST, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 08:03 EST, 6 November 2026

Thomas Tuchel has urged England fans to end their vendetta against Ben White.

Arsenal defender White was booed on his return to international football last March as England fans made it clear they hadn’t forgiven him for leaving the Qatar World Cup part way through the tournament.

Back in Tuchel’s squad for the first time since playing against Japan and Uruguay earlier this year, White could face the Wembley crowd against Croatia next Thursday.

The England manager said today: ‘We were not happy about it [the booing].

‘We understand the disappointment of the fans but it’s been expressed now and it should be a closed book.

‘Everybody can see the investment of Ben and the quality of Ben in terms of how he presented himself when he was with us.’

White scored England’s goal against Uruguay last March and then gave away the penalty from which the South Americans equalised in a 1-1 draw.

Thomas Tuchel has urged England fans to end their vendetta against Ben White (above)

The England boss called up the Arsenal defender again for the November international break

‘It was very eventful for him and not so easy,’ said Tuchel.

‘But when I spoke to him I could see the hunger. He deserves to be able to open a new chapter and he deserves positive feedback from the crowd’.

White went four years between caps after Gareth Southgate gave him the last cap of his tenure in March 2022. He left the Qatar World Cup after a difference of opinion with Southgate’s assistant Steve Holland and was never picked again.

#2298. Man United's secret plot to poach Sir Alex Ferguson from Aberdeen, 40 years on - from phone calls with a fake Scottish accent to clandestine meetings at M74 services on Bonfire Night

Published: 04:53 EST, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 08:07 EST, 6 November 2026

It is 40 years ago today since Manchester United appointed Sir Alex Ferguson as manager and ushered in the most successful era in the history of the club.

The parallels with the current situation at Old Trafford are striking. United have made their worst start to a league season since then, with just one win from their opening nine games, and Michael Carrick is fighting to save his job, just as Ron Atkinson was on the brink in November 1986.

United had rejected Big Ron’s offer to resign towards the end of the previous season. However, with the team only staying out of the relegation zone on goal difference, the club decided to sack him following a 4-1 League Cup defeat by Southampton.

Atkinson felt the seeds of a decision that would change the course of United’s history had been sown a few months earlier when he was working as a TV pundit at the World Cup in Mexico and Aberdeen boss Ferguson was in temporary charge of Scotland, following the sudden death of Jock Stein.

‘Lawrie McMenemy, also on the World Cup beat as a TV pundit, revealed he had heard Alex Ferguson had already been tapped up for my job,’ Atkinson later wrote in his autobiography.

‘I haven’t lost any sleep about the shenanigans surrounding my premature departure. I don’t blame Alex one bit. It’s all part of the management game. Somebody is inevitably waiting to nick your boots in this business.’

It is 40 years since Manchester United secretively lured Sir Alex Ferguson from Aberdeen

Club director Mike Edelson put on a fake Scottish accent to get through to Ferguson's office at Aberdeen, posing as Gordon Strachan's assistant

Atkinson’s suspicions were founded on a conversation between Ferguson and United director Sir Bobby Charlton in Mexico. Ferguson confirmed the chat, but disputes its significance.

‘That’s not true,’ Ferguson wrote, addressing Atkinson’s claim in his book Managing My Life. ‘Although Bobby did speak to me at the side of the pitch before Scotland’s fiasco of a game in Uruguay, the furthest he went was to ask that if I ever decided to move to England, I should let him know. I don’t think that could be considered an offer.

‘Apart from the brief contact with Bobby, there was nothing but rumour to link me with Old Trafford before November 5.’

Charlton’s recollection also suggests it was incidental. ‘I was working for the BBC and I went to watch Scotland train,’ he told Daily Mail Sport in 2011. ‘He saw me through the fence and came over to say hello. We only spoke for a minute or so, but I liked him.

‘I also recall going to Aberdeen once, to open a garage of all things. The people there were panicking. “You’re not here to take our manager, are you?” The whole city seemed to be in love with him. I stored that away, too.’

Atkinson viewed Charlton as an adversary in the boardroom. When he returned from Mexico keen to complete the signing of England centre back Terry Butcher from Ipswich, using money earmarked from the £2.3million sale of Mark Hughes to Barcelona, Charlton was the main opponent to the move.

Chairman Martin Edwards pulled the plug citing a lack of funds due, in part, to building a new museum at Old Trafford. ‘We’ll bring the museum up for a corner then!’ Atkinson replied.

Having decided a change of manager was needed, United favoured Ferguson over Terry Venables, but were anxious to make sure the 44-year-old wanted the job having drawn a blank with McMenemy, Bobby Robson and Ron Saunders before appointing Atkinson five years earlier.

Club director Martin Edwards spoke with Fergson and met him with a secret delegation at M74 services in Lanarkshire

Ferguson pictured with Graham Hogg (right) in his first week of training at Man United

Edwards, Charlton and fellow directors Mike Edelson and Maurice Watkins hatched a bold plan to get through to Ferguson’s office at Aberdeen by posing as Gordon Strachan’s accountant.

Edelson, who put on a Scottish accent to make the call, said: ‘It suddenly got very embarrassing when the receptionist started asking me personal questions about how my wife was. The other three were rolling around. But she put me through, Alex answered, I quickly explained who I really was and then put him on to Martin.’

Ferguson recalled taking the call in his office. ‘Martin Edwards gave me his number so I could ring him back and thus be assured of the authenticity of the call. I could scarcely contain myself as I dialled the Manchester number. Would I be interested in the job at Man United? Yes, I told him without hesitation.’

Ferguson immediately told his No 2 Archie Knox, but admitted that when he told his wife Cathy she was ‘devastated’ because the family were happy in Aberdeen.

It was her reluctance to move to London that had previously persuaded him to reject Arsenal and Tottenham but this time the opportunity was too good to refuse.

Ferguson arranged to meet the four-man delegation from United at 7pm that night at Hamilton Services on the M74 in Lanarkshire, where they would recognise each other using their car registrations. ‘It really was a case of flashing headlights,’ said Edelson.

Edwards got into Ferguson’s car and the others followed to the home of Cathy’s sister Bridget in the Bishopbriggs suburb of Glasgow.

Ferguson revealed he was offered a ‘disappointing’ salary that was less than he earned in Scotland and had no transfer budget. United also refused his request to buy the family home to ensure a quick sale, or pay back a £40,000 loan from Aberdeen, but he accepted the job anyway.

Ferguson surrounded by photographers in his first game in charge - they lost 2-0 against Oxford United at the Manor Ground

He became the most successful manager in English football history, winning 13 Premier League titles

United’s directors drove back to Manchester that night and Edwards sacked Atkinson at 10.30am on the morning of November 6. Edwards then caught a 2pm flight to Aberdeen with Watkins, who was also United’s solicitor, to conclude the deal with Aberdeen chairman Dick Donald.

Meanwhile, Atkinson was clearing his desk and saying his goodbyes at United before organising a farewell drinks party at his home in Rochdale which was attended by a number of United stars and went on a lot longer than planned.

‘It turned into an all-night rave with more than a hundred people packed into the house and lasted until 5am,’ Atkinson recalled.

There were a few sore heads when a nervous Ferguson gathered the players together on November 7. He was well aware of the drinking culture at Old Trafford and what he felt was Atkinson’s liberal attitude, based on information fed to him by Strachan, who had left Aberdeen for United two years earlier.

‘He cited all sorts of problems with the team, particularly the drinking and the alleged indifference of Big Ron to its damaging effects,’ Ferguson wrote.

‘The training, Gordon suggested, was a shambles, with nothing done until Ron had a session on his sunbed as a preliminary to joining in a small-sided practice game.’

Clamping down on the boozing was one of the first items on Ferguson’s agenda, but not before he banished bus driver Derek Sutton from sitting on the bench following his first game at Oxford 48 hours after being appointed.

It ended with a 2-0 defeat followed by a goalless draw at Norwich as Ferguson made an inauspicious start to a glittering career spanning 26-and-a-half years. His first game at Old Trafford brought a first win, beating QPR 1-0.

In a new book, Fergie: The Lost Tapes, author Wayne Barton has gone through 400 hours of recordings and unearthed a long-forgotten address by the new manager to United fans that day which has echoes of the farewell speech he made on the pitch in 2013.

‘Hello, Alex Ferguson here. I don’t need to tell you how important this day is to me. This is the greatest experience of my life, to be in front of this platform and this stage.

‘Since I’ve arrived here, I’ve been so aware of the traditions and success of this great club. My job is to make sure that success continues and to make us the greatest club in Britain.

‘The real supporters of Manchester United are the greatest in the world - don’t let anyone forget it.’

#2297. Milan, due vie per il centravanti: una a gennaio e l'altra in estate

Tra i colpi necessari che serviranno al Milan nel corso della prossima sessione di calciomercato, quella invernale nel mese di gennaio, rientra sicuramente l'acquisto di un centravanti. Per Gonçalo Ramos, che rimane il presente e il futuro del club vista anche la cifra spesa per acquistarlo in estate dal Paris Saint Germain, serve sia un po' di concorrenza, che un compagno che possa permettergli a un tempo di riposare ma anche di rendere al meglio. Nei piani estivi del Milan, Francesco Camarda doveva essere tutto questo: ma è un ragazzo ancora giovane e non pronto, su cui il Milan punta e che per questo verrà girato in prestito per giocare e formarsi adeguatamente.

Tornando al calciomercato in entrata e all'acquisto di un nuovo centravanti. Il Milan sta allestendo due vie per arrivare al nuovo attaccante, come riferisce il Corriere dello Sport questa mattina. Una riguarda il mercato di gennaio dove la priorità sarebbe quella di trovare un prestito a basso costo, magari per un calciatore pronto ma in esubero da una big europea. L'altra via è più estiva ed è inerente a Nicolò Tresoldi: l'attaccante classe 2004 piace moltissimo a Gerry Cardinale. Il suo costo attorno ai 40 milioni lo rende un colpo ancora più complicato per gennaio: ciononostante non è detto che il Milan possa decidere di anticipare la sua strategia, specialmente se non trovasse quel prestito a basso costo di cui si scriveva in precedenza.

#2295. Leeds have the longest unbeaten home run in the Premier League - from new transfer strategy and napping to choosing 'rough and ready' over 'comfy and nice', this is how they turned Elland Road into a fortress again

Published: 06:59 EST, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 09:23 EST, 6 November 2026

We will know a great deal more about the strength of Tottenham’s revival by mid-afternoon on Saturday. If Roberto De Zerbi’s men can escape one of the toughest venues in English football without too much damage, perhaps he really is on to something.

The Elland Road factor is no myth – Leeds United are currently on the longest unbeaten home run of any Premier League side, dating back eight games to a 1-0 reverse against Sunderland on March 3. Since Daniel Farke took over in 2023, only Arsenal, Manchester City and Liverpool have more points per game at home.

Farke's Leeds have won 44 of 69 games at Elland Road, losing only eight and scoring a whopping 141 goals.

After collecting a single point from matches at Sunderland and Bournemouth and failing to score in either, Leeds are desperate to be back in front of their own supporters.

Here, Daily Mail Sport looks at how one of England’s most evocative stadiums is baring its teeth again.

The last time Leeds were in the top flight, their home form was a huge part in their downfall. From 2021-23, Leeds lost nearly half of their home games (16 of 38) and won only nine.

The Elland Road factor is no myth. Since Daniel Farke became Leeds United boss in 2023, only Arsenal, Manchester City and Liverpool have more points per game at home

Farke's Leeds have won 44 of 69 games at Elland Road, losing only eight and scoring a whopping 141 goals. They are unbeaten there this season, and recently thrashed Newcastle 4-1

Farke and the players were able to set it right in the Championship, where Leeds won 35 of 47 in the following two campaigns including the play-off semi-final against Norwich. In the Premier League, it was always going to be a different story but Farke, his players and the supporters have responded to the challenge.

‘Since we’ve come back up, people realise how important the home record is and how we need to drive the team on,’ says Dan Moylan, co-owner of the Square Ball fan channel who has been going to Elland Road since 1982.

‘We know what we need to do to help the team. A lot of players will come to Elland Road and not fancy it. So many stadiums are too comfy and nice. We need it a bit rough and ready to get behind our team.

’Daniel Farke has galvanised the fanbase in a steady way. It’s not been spectacular, more of a steady building of momentum. There has been a lot of jeopardy with Leeds in recent seasons as they’ve gone through the divisions. There was a lot of anxiety in the Championship years but when the nerves were shredded, fans seemed to get behind the team more.’

Elland Road has always provoked strong reactions. Leeds supporters will always be hostile towards Gary Neville but the former Manchester United defender and Sky Sports pundit knows the value of these old-school sporting cathedrals.

‘Elland Road was unbelievable,’ Neville has said. 'There was a real nastiness and hostility to it, but it’s a proper football club with an incredible crowd. You always knew you were in for a brutal afternoon.’

Pep Guardiola saw the other side of the same coin. The 2021-22 campaign had few joys for Leeds, as they stayed up on the final day of the season.

‘Since I arrived, I heard many times about what Elland Road means,’ he said after Manchester City won 4-0 there in April 2022. 'At the end, 4-0 down, fighting to stay in the Premier League and singing, chanting, showing support. I can imagine how the people feel.’

Leeds prioritised top-flight experience after coming up in 2025, signing players such as Dominic Calvert-Lewin (centre) who can handle a rough atmosphere

Calvert-Lewin has scored 12 times at Elland Road since joining in summer 2025

To thrive at Elland Road you need players who can handle it when Leeds are struggling – and this was a significant part of their recruitment plan for the Premier League. Mindful of the failures at Elland Road between 2021 and 2023, Leeds took a different approach to recruitment after returning to the Premier League in 2025.

To make the most of their home, Leeds decided that top-flight experience was needed. In came players like Dominic Calvert-Lewin, Sean Longstaff and James Justin – battle-hardened Premier League footballers who know what it is to play in spicy atmospheres. These were men who would not wilt when Elland Road was at its most exacting.

The groundwork was laid in the Championship. For any home match outside the top flight, Leeds will always be heavy favourites – but this is double-edged. If a match against a low-profile club is still goalless after half an hour, fans start to groan. A couple of misplaced passes later and those groans turn to jeers.

It takes a certain kind of character to override those difficulties and in the second tier Leeds found it, racking up 190 points across the two seasons.

Before home games, Leeds have followed the same routine under Farke. They will meet at a hotel in the city on the morning of the match and travel by coach to Elland Road.

The pre-match meal and final tactical briefings will take place at the hotel and for later kick-offs, players are encouraged to have an afternoon nap. Sleep-deprived players with very young children may opt to stay in the hotel the night before the game. They will arrive at the ground 90 minutes before kick-off.

Elland Road is in the initial phase of a major redevelopment project that is designed to take the capacity from around 37,000 to 53,000. That means players now enter the stadium via a different route and can no longer be greeted by fans on their way in.

The mystique remains, though. Harry Wilson and Jayden Bogle were in the Derby side who won a spectacular play-off semi-final at Elland Road in May 2019. Both regard it as the best atmosphere they had ever played in and it played a part in their decision to join Leeds.

Harry Wilson and Jayden Bogle won a play-off semi-final at Elland Road with Derby in May 2019. Both regard it the best atmosphere they've played in

While stadium improvement is crucial, it is just as important that the essence of Elland Road does not slip away

Leeds lean heavily on their fanbase when recruiting. About 150,000 turned out in Leeds city centre to celebrate promotion 18 months ago and the club show videos of those scenes to transfer targets.

It is all part of the Leeds brand and while stadium improvement is crucial, it is just as important that the essence of Elland Road does not slip away.

‘It depends who can afford to get in,’ adds Moylan. ‘The club need to be careful not to price out supporters and they have to make sure young working-class fans can afford it.

‘I’ve seen the ground pretty raucous and animalistic, going back to the 1990s. Football has changed a lot over the years and the moving of kick-off times doesn’t help but on its day Elland Road now is as good as it’s ever been.’ Tottenham have been warned.

#2293. Bonera su Milan Futuro: "Non avevamo ben chiaro il livello della C: qualcuno pensava fosse più semplice"

Daniele Bonera, ex allenatore di Milan Futuro, nella stagione di esordio in Serie C per la squadra U23 rossonera, è stato intervistato da Fanpage ed è tornato su quel periodo e su quella esperienza professionale terminata con l'esonero: "Milan Futuro è stata una grande opportunità di crescita. Forse non avevamo ben chiaro, e mi ci metto dentro anch'io, quale fosse il livello della Serie C. lo lo avevo presente, ma qualcuno pensava fosse più semplice. La rosa era stata costruita con aspettative troppo alte rispetto a quello che poi è il campionato. Credo che quella struttura, in quel momento, non fosse pronta per affrontare quel tipo di campionato. A livello individuale, però, mi sono trovato molto bene. Iniziare a casa mia è stata una cosa positiva".

Continua Daniele Bonera sul progetto di Milan Futuro: "La difficoltà maggiore era capire quali giocatori avevi realmente a disposizione. Alcuni salivano in prima squadra, altri scendevano, altri ancora andavano in Primavera. Avevamo ragazzi che magari non giocavano con noi e poi risalivano. Dal punto di vista della crescita individuale, in alcune situazioni abbiamo lavorato molto bene. Dopo un paio d'anni abbiamo visto crescere il valore individuale di ragazzi come Camarda, Bartesaghi, Liberali e Jimenez. Il valore dei giocatori non era in discussione. lo credo che il problema fosse soprattutto strutturale: non sempre è l'allenatore il problema, ma il modo in cui si fanno le cose e la direzione che si vuole prendere".

#2292. Calò e la mancata convocazione in Nazionale, Mancini: "quando servirà lo faremo ma non perché ce lo dicono i procuratori"

Il ct riconosce le prestazioni del centrocampista del Frosinone, ma chiarisce il criterio seguito per le convocazioni.

Giacomo Calò è seguito dalla Nazionale, ma Roberto Mancini non lo ha convocato per il raduno di Coverciano. Il commissario tecnico riconosce il buon momento del centrocampista del Frosinone, allenato da Massimiliano Alvini. Secondo quanto riportato da figc, A 29 anni, però, Calò non rientra tra i giocatori che il ct vuole osservare in questa fase, dedicata soprattutto ai più giovani.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Gli Azzurri si sono ritrovati a Coverciano la sera dell’8 novembre 2026. Nel pomeriggio del 9 novembre è prevista la prima seduta di allenamento verso le ultime due partite del girone di Nations League, contro Francia e Belgio. Nella conferenza stampa di inizio raduno, Mancini ha affrontato anche il tema delle convocazioni e delle prospettive della squadra. La FIGC riporta le sue dichiarazioni.

Mancini considera Calò uno dei giocatori che stanno facendo bene e che la Nazionale tiene sotto osservazione. Il ct ha aggiunto che nel Frosinone ci sono altri tre o quattro elementi validi. La scelta per questo raduno, tuttavia, segue un criterio diverso: «Ma non possiamo chiamare giocatori di 29/30 anni in questa fase, quando servirà lo faremo ma non perché ce lo dicono i procuratori».

Il tecnico ha allargato il discorso allo spazio concesso ai giovani in Serie A. A suo giudizio, la situazione è rimasta più o meno la stessa: alcuni club li impiegano, altri no. Per la Nazionale, ha spiegato, resta l’obbligo di cercare ragazzi da convocare e conoscere direttamente, anche quando sono molto giovani. L’intento è valutarne le qualità tecniche in vista della loro possibile crescita.

La linea seguita dal ct riguarda anche alcuni campioni d’Europa assenti dall’elenco, tra cui Bernardeschi, Chiesa e Cristante. Mancini conosce già Bernardeschi e Cristante e ha detto che potrà richiamarli quando ne avrà bisogno. Con Chiesa ha parlato più volte, anche di recente: il giocatore ora non sta giocando, deve ritrovare la condizione e, secondo il ct, a 29 anni può tornare presto in Nazionale.

Mancini ha confermato sei dei nove volti nuovi visti nella finestra di settembre e ottobre: Ghilardi, Kayode, Romano, Doumbia, Sebastiano Esposito e Vergara. Ha richiamato Ahanor e accolto dall’Under 21 Pisilli e Koleosho. Parlando del loro passaggio, ha ricordato il buon rapporto con Baldini e gli ha augurato di vincere l’Europeo.

Tra le prime convocazioni figurano due giocatori del Como di Fabregas: Mattia Liberali, classe 2007, e Riccardo Cassano, classe 2009. Nell’elenco c’è anche Alphadjo Cisse, attaccante del Milan nato nel 2006. La sua chiamata era nell’aria: l’8 novembre ha segnato per la quinta volta in Serie A ed è il miglior marcatore rossonero. Completano le novità il difensore dell’Udinese Matteo Palma e Simone Lontani, del Parma, entrambi classe 2008.

Palma, Liberali e Cisse resteranno con il gruppo per tutto il raduno. Cassano e Lontani si alleneranno per alcuni giorni a Coverciano, poi raggiungeranno rispettivamente le Nazionali Under 18 e Under 19. La prima è impegnata nel Mondiale al via il 19 novembre in Qatar; la seconda nella fase delle qualificazioni europee in programma dall’11 al 17 novembre nelle Marche. Mancini li ha chiamati anche per conoscerli e osservarli da vicino.

Il ct ha detto che tutti i giovani presenti gli hanno dato buone sensazioni. Di Liberali ha sottolineato le qualità e il percorso nelle giovanili, pur ricordando le poche presenze in Serie A. Lontani lo aveva colpito già alla prima di campionato: a suo giudizio è migliorato partita dopo partita e il gol contro l’Atalanta ne conferma personalità e doti tecniche.

Palma, che era in bilico tra Italia e Germania, può diventare secondo Mancini un difensore molto bravo. Cisse può ricoprire entrambi i ruoli d’attacco e il ct vuole vederlo al lavoro. Cassano è un regista di centrocampo: deve crescere fisicamente, ma Mancini ne apprezza la lettura del gioco e ritiene che possa diventare importante per il futuro azzurro.

Un’altra scelta tra Italia e Germania riguarda Nicolò Tresoldi. Mancini e Ranieri hanno parlato con lui, ma il ct ha ribadito che la decisione spetta al giocatore. Tresoldi è nato in Italia e suo padre ha giocato per la Nazionale, vincendo l’Europeo Under 21. Per Mancini ci sono possibilità che scelga gli Azzurri, con i quali potrebbe avere un ruolo importante.

Il selezionatore considera la sua esclusione la decisione più difficile e richiama la concorrenza a centrocampo.

L’Inter quanto è migliorabile secondo lei? E dove.

#2288. Thomas Tuchel tells Cole Palmer he 'misses too many opportunities' to play for England after leaving Chelsea star out of his latest squad

By JAMES DUTTON, DEPUTY SPORTS NEWS EDITOR and KIERAN GILL, MAIL SPORT REPORTER

Published: 06:46 EST, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 09:39 EST, 6 November 2026

Thomas Tuchel has warned Cole Palmer he 'misses too many opportunities' to play for England, after leaving him out of his latest squad.

The Chelsea star is a high-profile omission ahead of upcoming Nations League games against Croatia and Spain, having withdrawn from duty during the last international break with a minor muscular injury.

England were assured Palmer is 100 per cent fit and ready when they contacted Chelsea this week – with his omission purely down to a selection decision from Tuchel and not to do with any fitness issues.

The German said on Friday: 'I guess everyone is desperate to play for England and Cole is no exception.

'We are speaking now about a player that is not in camp. It counts when you are in camp and I just said last time that he misses too many opportunities to impress.

'It was a long camp, it was two and a half weeks and the players and the boys who were in camp built something extraordinary.

Thomas Tuchel has warned Cole Palmer he misses too many opportunities to play for England

Palmer is a high-profile omission from Tuchel's squad for England's upcoming Nations League games

He withdrew from the last squad, leaving boss Thomas Tuchel questioning his commitment

'That's why the credit goes to them, the trust is for them. If you are in camp, this is your chance to impress.

'So it's not personal and of course we will never assume that Cole is not in the mindset to come and desperate to play for his country, but the competition is on and the players who were in camp did an amazing job and kept their place.'

Last month Tuchel appeared to question Palmer's desire to represent his country, warning he 'misses way too many camps' as the 24-year-old skipped all four England fixtures in September and October.

Palmer had played a full 90 for Chelsea away at Bournemouth before that break, which he then used to fly abroad to the Caribbean with his girlfriend, Olivia Holder, after withdrawing from England duty.

He was accompanied by a personal rehab coach on that trip, and was provided with an injury-specific training and recovery plan by his club.

Palmer started in Chelsea's first fixture when the Premier League returned at home to Brentford with Blues boss Xabi Alonso insisting they were right to take care of their star player themselves.

It has been described as standard procedure for England to make medical checks on players with clubs. While it does not guarantee a call-up, it indicates how Tuchel has been weighing up offering Palmer another chance as he names his next squad on Friday morning.

Chelsea boss Xabi Alonso (right) said he had not spoken with Palmer about his England omission

Asked if he had spoken with Palmer about his England omission, Alonso said: 'We have not spoken about that. We have been talking about our game on Sunday. We are very happy with Cole.

'He's enjoying his game, he's connecting very well with the other attackers, he's helping the team. I'm very happy with his quality, his attitude, and his impact.

'We have not talked about that (England). We are focused on Chelsea things, how to do better, how to help him, how he can become more and more influential in the game.

'We speak about Chelsea. We speak about us. We have enough on our plate. My impact area is my control area, and that's what we talk about.

'He needs to focus on his thing. His thing is to play football, to enjoy his game, with us, with Chelsea, to connect with his team-mates.

'I would never tell (a player to withdraw). When a player is fully fit, he is selectable. When he is not fit, we try to give the national team the reasonable information to decide together, because sometimes it's not good for the player.

'For example, Anselmino, he has been called up for Argentina, they asked us, he has been training for the last 10 days better, and for him, it's very important to have his first call-up.'

#2287. Cancellieri, meno spazio alla Lazio: Fiorentina attenta alla scadenza

Una cessione a gennaio consentirebbe ai biancocelesti di ricavare una somma dal cartellino dell'esterno.

Matteo Cancellieri potrebbe tornare nei piani della Fiorentina per il mercato invernale. Secondo quanto riportato da FiorentinaUno, L'esterno offensivo della Lazio, che nelle ultime settimane ha trovato poco spazio, sarebbe nuovamente seguito da Fabio Paratici. Un'eventuale operazione a gennaio resta un'ipotesi, legata anche alle scelte del club biancoceleste.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il futuro a Roma del calciatore italiano, classe 2002, non appare scontato. Cancellieri non sembra più occupare una posizione centrale nelle scelte tecniche della Lazio e sta attraversando una fase complicata, nella quale giocare con continuità è diventato più difficile.

Alla situazione in campo si aggiunge quella contrattuale: l'accordo con i biancocelesti scade il 30 giugno 2027. Senza un rinnovo, la società dovrà decidere come affrontare gli ultimi mesi del rapporto. Anche il giocatore sarà chiamato a valutare quale soluzione possa offrirgli maggiore continuità.

Paratici conosce il profilo di Cancellieri e potrebbe scegliere tra due possibilità. La prima è provare ad anticipare l'operazione a gennaio, cercando un'intesa con la Lazio. L'altra è aspettare la fine della stagione per tentare di ingaggiare l'esterno a parametro zero.

Attendere consentirebbe alla Fiorentina di non sostenere il costo del cartellino, ma potrebbe esporla alla concorrenza di altre società. Non è la prima volta che il nome di Cancellieri viene associato ai viola: nelle precedenti finestre di mercato il club toscano aveva già mostrato interesse, considerandolo una possibile alternativa per il reparto offensivo.

Tra le caratteristiche prese in considerazione c'è la sua duttilità. Cancellieri può giocare soprattutto sulla corsia destra, ma anche in altre posizioni dell'attacco: una possibilità utile nella costruzione della rosa viola, che potrebbe cambiare volto.

#2286. Ausilio sente sua questa Inter. Ora nella testa dell'ex ds c'è un'idea ben precisa

Ausilio sente sua questa Inter. Ora nella testa dell'ex ds c'è un'idea ben precisa

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Tra i protagonisti al Social Football Summit 2026 di Torino, Piero Ausilio è tornato a parlare dopo l'addio all'Inter. L'ex dirigente nerazzurro ha chiuso la sua avventura a Milano dopo ben 28 anni. "Un amore vissuto intensamente potrà forse sbiadire col tempo, ma difficilmente si consumerà del tutto. Di certo non dopo appena due mesi da una separazione consensuale che le parti, per quanto repentina, hanno voluto fosse il meno traumatica possibile. Piero Ausilio è sinceramente appagato per aver chiuso la sua lunga e vincente parentesi all’Inter nel modo in cui aveva sempre immaginato", sottolinea il Corriere dello Sport.

"Ausilio continua a vivere con la passione di sempre le partite di una squadra che ha costruito dalla prima all’ultima operazione, sentendola quindi ancora legittimamente anche una sua creatura. La fine della lunga storia ha ridefinito la quotidianità, ma non ha cancellato almeno la maggior parte dei legami costruiti negli anni".

"Parallelamente, è giusto anche guardarsi intorno. Senza la frenesia di voler rientrare a ogni costo, ma con la consapevolezza di poter dare tanto. E l’adrenalina giusta per vivere una nuova sfida, magari ad altre latitudini. Nella testa di Ausilio ora c’è un’idea ben precisa ed è lui stesso a confermarlo: «Dopo tanti anni in un'unica squadra, il desiderio che ho è di misurarmi in un campionato diverso e la Premier League rappresenta il progetto più affascinante. Questo non vuol dire che ci sia l'opportunità di andarci dall'oggi al domani, però è sicuramente qualcosa che mi interessa e la prenderei in considerazione».

#2285. Generali Stadium, Sala chiaro: "Non intendo avere ripensamenti""

Nella giornata di ieri sono emerse ulteriori novità in merito al progetto del Generali Stadium di Milan e Inter. In breve, il Consiglio Comunale di Milano ha bloccato l'attuale valutazione ambientale in corso dell'impianto per aver la possibilità di condurne una più approfondita. Un tema che ha scatenato reazioni contrastanti e che ha anche sollecitato il parere del Sindaco di Milano Giuseppe Sala che ieri ha rilasciato un commento che non dà spazio a molte interpretazioni.

Il commento del sindaco Giuseppe Sala in merito alle ultime indiscrezioni in merito al progetto del Generali Stadium di Milan e Inter: "Io rispetto l’opinione del Consiglio comunale ma l’aula rispetterà il fatto che io non intendo avere ripensamenti su questo processo. Ne parleremo anche in giunta, ma non intendo avere ripensamenti. Ne abbiamo parlato tre anni onestamente, per cui il Pd faccia quello che ritiene. Io, ripeto, faccio quello che ritengo e questo vuol dire continuare ad essere un uomo coerente, che ha impostato le cose in un certo modo dopo lunghissime discussioni"

#2283. FCINTER1908 / Inter, Lautaro verso il rientro: quando tornerà in gruppo

FCINTER1908 / Inter, Lautaro verso il rientro: quando tornerà in gruppo

Il rientro di Lautaro Martinez si avvicina, ma servirà ancora un po' di pazienza prima di rivedere il capitano dell'Inter nelle migliori condizioni. L'attaccante argentino sta proseguendo il percorso di recupero dall'infortunio muscolare e ha nel mirino la trasferta contro l'Atalanta, in programma domenica 22 novembre.
